PATH - documentation\architectures\agentic-orchestration-and-tools.md
CODE:
# VIGIL — Agentic Orchestration, Specialized Agents & Forensic Tools Specification

**Status:** Completed Baseline Implementation (Layer 2)  
**Scope:** `AgenticOrchestrator`, `InvestigationPlanner`, Specialized Agents (`VisualAgent`, `AudioAgent`, `TemporalCorrelationAgent`, `EvidenceVerificationAgent`, `ReportWriterAgent`), Forensic Tool Workers, Free Model Adapters, and Referential Repository.

---

## 1. System Overview & Alignment with Workflow Diagram

The VIGIL agentic orchestration layer realizes the core intelligence and workflow described in `VIGIL_vison_modelContext.txt`. It connects the strict Pydantic domain schemas (Layer 1) with autonomous reasoning, specialized analysis agents, multi-modal tools, and free-tier LLMs.

### Architectural Mapping

```
                                      [ Investigator / User ]
                                                 │
                                                 ▼
                                     [ API / Application Layer ]
                                                 │
                                                 ▼
                                     [ Investigation Planner ]
                                                 │
                                                 ▼
                                    [ Agentic Orchestrator ]
                     (State Machine, Lifecycle Transitions, Checkpoints)
                                                 │
            ┌──────────────────┬─────────────────┼──────────────────┬──────────────────┐
            ▼                  ▼                 ▼                  ▼                  ▼
     [ Visual Agent ]   [ Audio Agent ]   [ Temporal Agent ] [ Verif. Agent ]  [ Report Agent ]
            │                  │                 │                  │                  │
            ▼                  ▼                 ▼                  ▼                  ▼
      Vision Tools        Audio Tools      Temporal Tools     Evidence Tools     Reporting Tools
     (FrameExtractor)  (AudioTranscriber) (EventCorrelator)  (EvidenceAssessor) (ReportGenerator)
     (VisualDetector)                                        (CoverageTracker)
            │                  │                 │                  │                  │
            └──────────────────┴────────┬────────┴──────────────────┴──────────────────┘
                                        ▼
                             [ Free Model Adapters ]
                     (Gemini 2.0 Flash, Groq Llama 3.3, Ollama)
                                        │
                                        ▼
                         [ Investigation Repository ]
                   (Artifacts, Observations, Events, Reports)
```

---

## 2. Specialized Agents Specification

### 2.1 `AgenticOrchestrator` (`LeadInvestigatorAgent`)
- **Module:** `src/vigil/agent/investigator.py`
- **Role:** Owns the multi-phase execution loop, state tracking, and error recovery.
- **Key Responsibilities:**
  1. Registers the initial `Investigation` into `InvestigationRepository`.
  2. Initializes `InvestigationState` in `CREATED` status.
  3. Obtains the decomposed forensic task sequence from `InvestigationPlanner`.
  4. Coordinates specialized agents and routes tasks through `OrchestrationService`.
  5. Accumulates generated `Observation` and `Event` IDs into the immutable `InvestigationState`.
  6. Completes the investigation upon successful task completion, transitioning to `COMPLETED`.

### 2.2 `InvestigationPlanner`
- **Module:** `src/vigil/agent/planner.py`
- **Role:** Decomposes investigation title, objective, scope, and media assets into actionable `PlannedTask` instances.
- **Workflow Phases Planned:**
  1. `frame_sampling`: Sample keyframes across media timeline.
  2. `visual_analysis`: Detect persons, vehicles, objects, and anomalies.
  3. `audio_transcription`: Extract acoustic cues and spoken dialogue.
  4. `event_correlation`: Group observations into chronological incidents and event links.
  5. `evidence_assessment`: Evaluate evidence quality, support level, and uncertainty.
  6. `coverage_evaluation`: Verify footage examination intervals.
  7. `report_generation`: Compile findings into a structured `Report`.

### 2.3 `VisualAgent`
- **Module:** `src/vigil/agent/specialized/visual_agent.py`
- **Role:** Coordinates frame extraction and visual multi-modal analysis.
- **Tools Invoked:** `FrameExtractorWorker` (`frame_sampling`), `VisualDetectorWorker` (`visual_analysis`).

### 2.4 `AudioAgent`
- **Module:** `src/vigil/agent/specialized/audio_agent.py`
- **Role:** Coordinates acoustic transcription, speech analysis, and sound event detection.
- **Tools Invoked:** `AudioTranscriberWorker` (`audio_transcription`).

### 2.5 `TemporalCorrelationAgent`
- **Module:** `src/vigil/agent/specialized/correlation_agent.py`
- **Role:** Discovers chronological sequences, overlapping activities, and multi-camera associations.
- **Tools Invoked:** `EventCorrelatorWorker` (`event_correlation`).
- **Domain Outputs:** Produces `Event` and `EventLink` (`before`, `after`, `overlaps`, `related_to`, `possible_same_incident`).

### 2.6 `EvidenceVerificationAgent`
- **Module:** `src/vigil/agent/specialized/verification_agent.py`
- **Role:** Assesses evidence quality, support level, uncertainty, and footage coverage completeness.
- **Tools Invoked:** `EvidenceAssessorWorker` (`evidence_assessment`), `CoverageTrackerWorker` (`coverage_evaluation`).
- **Domain Outputs:** Produces `EvidenceAssessment` and `CoverageRecord`.

### 2.7 `ReportWriterAgent`
- **Module:** `src/vigil/agent/specialized/report_writer_agent.py`
- **Role:** Synthesizes verified findings, timelines, and assessments into a formal forensic document.
- **Tools Invoked:** `ReportGeneratorWorker` (`report_generation`).
- **Domain Outputs:** Produces `Report`.

---

## 3. Forensic Tools (`src/vigil/tools/`)

All workers conform strictly to the `TaskWorker` protocol (`execute(request: TaskRequest) -> TaskResult`):

| Worker Class | Task Type | Input References | Generated Outputs | Key Validations |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `FrameExtractorWorker` | `frame_sampling`<br>`clip_extraction` | `MediaAsset` IDs | `Artifact` IDs | Frame interval bounds, 64-hex SHA-256 checksums, positive durations. |
| `VisualDetectorWorker` | `visual_analysis` | `Artifact` IDs | `Observation` IDs, `Entity` IDs | Media modality = `image`, `Provenance` record with model name, non-blank descriptions. |
| `AudioTranscriberWorker` | `audio_transcription` | `MediaAsset` / `Artifact` IDs | `Observation` IDs | Media modality = `audio`, audio provenance. |
| `EventCorrelatorWorker` | `event_correlation` | `Observation` IDs | `Event` IDs, `EventLink` IDs | Supporting observations ≥ 1, no self-referential links, timezone-aware event times. |
| `EvidenceAssessorWorker` | `evidence_assessment` | `Observation` / `Event` IDs | `EvidenceAssessment` IDs | Ratings (`insufficient`, `low`, `moderate`, `high`), distinct from model confidence. |
| `CoverageTrackerWorker` | `coverage_evaluation` | `SourceReference` / `MediaAsset` IDs | `CoverageRecord` IDs | Paired start/end timestamps, `examined` status requires `examined_at`. |
| `ReportGeneratorWorker` | `report_generation` | None (reads repo) | `Report` IDs | Non-blank title, summary, and links to all supporting records. |

---

## 4. Free-Tier Model Adapters (`src/vigil/models/`)

The architecture provides multi-provider LLM support through standard library HTTP (`urllib.request`), requiring zero heavy third-party dependencies:

1. **Google Gemini Free Tier (`GeminiClient`)**:
   - Endpoint: `https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta`
   - Models: `gemini-2.0-flash`, `gemini-1.5-flash`
   - Free Quota: 15 RPM, 1M TPM, 1,500 RPD.
2. **Groq Cloud Free Tier (`GroqClient`)**:
   - Endpoint: `https://api.groq.com/openai/v1`
   - Models: `llama-3.3-70b-versatile`, `llama-3.2-11b-vision-preview`, `whisper-large-v3`
3. **Local & Offline (`OpenAICompatibleClient`)**:
   - Endpoint: `http://localhost:11434/v1` (Ollama) or OpenRouter free models (`:free`)
4. **Mock Provider (`MockLLMClient`)**:
   - Deterministic, high-fidelity mock client for automated testing and CI/CD without API keys.

---

## 5. Storage & Referential Integrity (`src/vigil/storage/`)

- Implements `InvestigationRepository` with `threading.RLock`.
- Provides lookup, persistence, and cross-record existence checks (`validate_references_exist`) ensuring referential integrity without polluting Pydantic model schemas.

---

## 6. Next Plan of Action (Phase 3 Roadmap)

According to the workflow diagram in `VIGIL_vison_modelContext.txt`, the next logical implementation phases are:

### Phase 3: Media Ingestion, Indexing & Storage Engine
1. **Actual Video/Audio Ingestion Worker**:
   - Integrate lightweight media decoding (e.g. OpenCV / PyAV / ffmpeg wrapper) to extract actual image frames from `.mp4`/`.avi` files.
   - Demux audio tracks into `.wav` segments for Whisper transcription.
2. **Metadata Extractor**:
   - Extract real video FPS, resolution, codec, duration, and container timestamps.
3. **Playable Incident Clip Generator**:
   - Slice short video clips (`Artifact(artifact_type="clip")`) around detected event intervals for human investigator playback.
4. **Persistent Database Layer**:
   - Provide a SQLite/PostgreSQL implementation of `InvestigationRepository` so long-running investigations survive process restarts.

### Phase 4: Application Services & API Layer
1. **FastAPI Endpoints**:
   - `POST /investigations`: Submit video footage and investigation objectives.
   - `GET /investigations/{id}/state`: Stream or poll real-time task progress.
   - `GET /investigations/{id}/report`: Fetch the compiled forensic report.
2. **WebSocket Telemetry**:
   - Push live agent reasoning steps, detected observations, and coverage updates.

### Phase 5: Human Review & Audit Layer
1. **Investigator Confirmation Workflow**:
   - Endpoint for human review: approve, reject, or edit proposed `Event` records (`Event.status = "confirmed"` or `"rejected"`).
2. **Report Versioning & Export**:
   - Export reports to standardized formats (PDF, Markdown, HTML) with embedded clip links and audit signatures.

PATH - documentation\architectures\evidence-and-interpretation-schemas.md
CODE:
# VIGIL Layer 1 — Evidence and Interpretation Schema Specification

**Status:** Initial implementation specification  
**Scope:** Provenance, Observation, Entity, Event, EventLink and EvidenceAssessment

## 1. Provenance

**Python file:** `src/vigil/domain/provenance.py`

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `producer_type` | Literal | Yes | Category of the model, tool, rule, agent or human that produced the result. |
| `producer_name` | `str` | Yes | Name of the producing component or person. |
| `producer_version` | `str \| None` | No | Version of the producing component, if known. |
| `method` | `str` | Yes | Operation or analysis method used to produce the result. |
| `input_artifact_ids` | `list[str]` | No | IDs of derived artifacts consumed during analysis. Defaults to an empty list. |
| `parent_observation_ids` | `list[str]` | No | IDs of earlier observations used to derive or refine the result. Defaults to an empty list. |
| `created_at` | `datetime` | Yes | Timezone-aware timestamp when the result was produced; defaults to UTC now. |

**Validation rules**

- Producer name and method must not be blank.
- References must not be blank or duplicated.
- Creation timestamps must be timezone-aware.
- References must resolve to existing records when validated by the service layer.

## 2. Observation

**Python file:** `src/vigil/domain/observation.py`

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `observation_id` | `str` | Yes | Stable unique identifier for the observation. |
| `modality` | Literal | Yes | Media modality: video, audio or image. |
| `observation_type` | `str` | Yes | Kind of finding, such as person detection, motion or audio transcription. |
| `description` | `str` | Yes | Human-readable description of the finding. |
| `source_references` | `list[SourceReference]` | Yes | One or more source-media intervals supporting the finding. |
| `artifact_ids` | `list[str]` | No | IDs of associated derived artifacts. Defaults to an empty list. |
| `confidence` | `float \| None` | No | Optional producer-reported score from 0 to 1; not necessarily a calibrated probability. |
| `provenance` | `Provenance` | Yes | Information about how the observation was produced. |

**Validation rules**

- The ID, type and description must not be blank.
- At least one source reference is required.
- Confidence must be finite and within 0–1.
- Artifact references must not be blank or duplicated.
- Source intervals and artifact references must be checked against existing records by the service layer.

## 3. Entity

**Python file:** `src/vigil/domain/entity.py`

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `entity_id` | `str` | Yes | Stable identifier for a detected or tracked entity. |
| `entity_type` | Literal | Yes | Category: person, vehicle, animal, object or unknown. |
| `label` | `str \| None` | No | Optional human-readable label; not necessarily a verified identity. |
| `associated_observation_ids` | `list[str]` | Yes | IDs of observations associated with this entity; at least one is required. |
| `attributes` | `dict[str, str \| int \| float \| bool \| None]` | No | Optional structured attributes such as clothing color or vehicle type. Defaults to an empty dictionary. |

**Validation rules**

- Entity IDs must not be blank.
- At least one associated observation is required.
- Observation references must not be blank or duplicated.
- Attribute names must not be blank.
- Numeric attribute values must be finite.
- Association and identity consistency must be evaluated by the service layer.

## 4. Event

**Python file:** `src/vigil/domain/event.py`

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `event_id` | `str` | Yes | Stable unique identifier for the event. |
| `event_type` | `str` | Yes | Category of occurrence, such as restricted-area entry. |
| `description` | `str` | Yes | Human-readable description of the occurrence. |
| `observation_ids` | `list[str]` | Yes | Supporting observation IDs; at least one is required. |
| `entity_ids` | `list[str]` | No | Associated entity IDs. Defaults to an empty list. |
| `status` | Literal | Yes | Review state: proposed, under review, confirmed or rejected. Defaults to proposed. |
| `start_time` | `datetime \| None` | No | Optional timezone-aware estimated wall-clock start time. |
| `end_time` | `datetime \| None` | No | Optional timezone-aware estimated wall-clock end time. |

**Validation rules**

- Event IDs, types and descriptions must not be blank.
- At least one supporting observation is required.
- Observation and entity references must not be blank or duplicated.
- Timestamps must be timezone-aware when supplied.
- End time must not precede start time when both are supplied.
- Event references must resolve to existing records.
- Confirmation status must not be treated as proof without an established verification process.

## 5. EventLink

**Python file:** `src/vigil/domain/event_link.py`

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `event_link_id` | `str` | Yes | Stable unique identifier for the relationship. |
| `from_event_id` | `str` | Yes | ID of the source event. |
| `to_event_id` | `str` | Yes | ID of the destination event. |
| `relationship_type` | Literal | Yes | Relationship: before, after, overlaps, related to or possible same incident. |
| `rationale` | `str` | Yes | Explanation for recording the relationship. |
| `confidence` | `float \| None` | No | Optional producer-reported confidence score between 0 and 1. |
| `created_at` | `datetime` | Yes | Timezone-aware relationship creation timestamp; defaults to UTC now. |

**Validation rules**

- Event IDs and rationale must not be blank.
- Source and destination events must differ.
- Confidence must be finite and within 0–1.
- Creation timestamps must be timezone-aware.
- Both event references must resolve to existing events.
- Relationship direction and inverse semantics must be documented before implementing correlation rules.
- Temporal association must not automatically imply causation.

## 6. EvidenceAssessment

**Python file:** `src/vigil/domain/evidence_assessment.py`

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `assessment_id` | `str` | Yes | Stable unique identifier for the assessment. |
| `target_type` | Literal | Yes | Type of assessed record: observation or event. |
| `target_id` | `str` | Yes | ID of the record being assessed. |
| `assessment_type` | Literal | Yes | Assessment dimension: evidence quality, support level or uncertainty. |
| `rating` | Literal | Yes | Qualitative rating: insufficient, low, moderate or high. |
| `rationale` | `str` | Yes | Explanation supporting the assessment. |
| `assessed_by` | `str` | Yes | Name or identifier of the assessor or assessment tool. |
| `created_at` | `datetime` | Yes | Timezone-aware assessment timestamp; defaults to UTC now. |

**Validation rules**

- Assessment ID, target ID, rationale and assessor must not be blank.
- Target type and assessment type must use declared values.
- The target must exist and match the declared target type.
- Ratings must be interpreted according to documented assessment criteria.
- Assessments must remain distinct from model confidence scores.

## Shared conventions

- All schema models inherit from the shared `DomainModel`.
- Unknown information must remain optional rather than being fabricated.
- IDs identify records; they do not prove that the referenced records exist.
- Cross-record existence and integrity checks belong in a repository or service layer.
- Source references preserve media-relative time; wall-clock time is optional and timezone-aware.
- Changes to fields, types, relationships or validation rules require corresponding tests and documentation updates.

PATH - documentation\architectures\execution-and-orchestration-schemas.md
CODE:
# VIGIL Layer 1 — Execution and Orchestration Schema Specification

**Status:** Initial implementation specification  
**Scope:** TaskRequest, TaskResult, TaskStatus, ErrorDetail, ModelOutput and InvestigationState

## 1. TaskRequest

**Python file:** `src/vigil/orchestration/task_types.py`

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `task_id` | `str` | Yes | Stable unique identifier for the task. |
| `task_type` | `str` | Yes | Operation to perform, such as frame sampling or visual analysis. |
| `input_refs` | `list[str]` | No | IDs of media assets, artifacts or other inputs. Defaults to an empty list. |
| `parameters` | `dict[str, JsonValue]` | No | JSON-compatible parameters controlling the operation. Defaults to an empty dictionary. |
| `requested_by` | `str \| None` | No | Identifier of the requesting agent or component, if known. |
| `created_at` | `datetime` | Yes | Timezone-aware task creation timestamp; defaults to UTC now. |

**Validation rules**

- Task IDs and task types must not be blank.
- Input references must not be blank or duplicated.
- Creation timestamps must be timezone-aware.
- Parameters must contain JSON-compatible values.
- Referenced inputs must be resolved by the service or repository layer.

## 2. TaskStatus

**Python file:** `src/vigil/orchestration/task_types.py`

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `pending` | Task is waiting to execute. |
| `running` | Task is currently executing. |
| `completed` | Task completed successfully. |
| `partial` | Task produced partial results but did not complete all requested work. |
| `failed` | Task execution failed. |

These values describe execution, not the truth or confidence of an analysis finding.

## 3. ErrorDetail

**Python file:** `src/vigil/orchestration/task_types.py`

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `code` | `str` | Yes | Stable machine-readable error code. |
| `message` | `str` | Yes | Human-readable explanation of the error. |
| `retryable` | `bool` | Yes | Indicates whether retrying may succeed. |
| `retry_after_seconds` | `float \| None` | No | Optional non-negative delay before retrying. |

**Validation rules**

- Error code and message must not be blank.
- Retry delays must be finite and non-negative.
- A retry delay may be supplied only when the error is retryable.
- Retryability should be defined consistently for each error code.

## 4. TaskResult

**Python file:** `src/vigil/orchestration/task_types.py`

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `task_id` | `str` | Yes | Identifier of the task producing the result. |
| `status` | `TaskStatus` | Yes | Execution lifecycle status. |
| `output_refs` | `list[str]` | No | IDs of outputs such as observations or artifacts. Defaults to an empty list. |
| `error` | `ErrorDetail \| None` | No | Structured execution error, when applicable. |
| `started_at` | `datetime \| None` | No | Timezone-aware execution start time, if known. |
| `completed_at` | `datetime \| None` | No | Timezone-aware execution end time, if known. |

**Validation rules**

- Task ID must not be blank.
- Output references must not be blank or duplicated.
- Execution timestamps must be timezone-aware.
- Completion time must not precede start time.
- Failed results require error details.
- Completed results must not contain an error.
- Partial results may include an error describing incomplete work.

## 5. ModelOutput

**Python file:** `src/vigil/orchestration/task_types.py`

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `output_id` | `str` | Yes | Stable unique identifier for the model or tool output. |
| `producer_name` | `str` | Yes | Name of the model or tool producing the output. |
| `output_type` | `str` | Yes | Type of structured output, such as detection or transcription. |
| `payload` | `dict[str, JsonValue]` | Yes | JSON-compatible structured result. |
| `confidence` | `float \| None` | No | Optional producer-reported score between 0 and 1. |
| `artifact_ids` | `list[str]` | No | IDs of associated artifacts. Defaults to an empty list. |
| `created_at` | `datetime` | Yes | Timezone-aware output creation timestamp; defaults to UTC now. |

**Validation rules**

- Required identifiers and descriptive fields must not be blank.
- Payload values must be JSON-compatible.
- Confidence must be finite and within 0–1.
- Artifact references must not be blank or duplicated.
- Creation timestamps must be timezone-aware.
- Tool-specific payload structures must be validated by their respective contracts.

## 6. InvestigationState

**Python file:** `src/vigil/orchestration/investigation_state.py`

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `investigation_id` | `str` | Yes | Stable identifier for the investigation workflow. |
| `status` | `InvestigationStatus` | Yes | Lifecycle state: created, running, paused, completed or failed. Defaults to created. |
| `task_ids` | `list[str]` | No | Associated task IDs. Defaults to an empty list. |
| `observation_ids` | `list[str]` | No | Observation IDs produced during the investigation. Defaults to an empty list. |
| `event_ids` | `list[str]` | No | Event IDs identified during the investigation. Defaults to an empty list. |
| `started_at` | `datetime \| None` | No | Timezone-aware investigation start time. |
| `updated_at` | `datetime` | Yes | Timezone-aware last-update timestamp; defaults to UTC now. |
| `completed_at` | `datetime \| None` | No | Timezone-aware terminal-state timestamp. |

**Validation rules**

- Investigation IDs must not be blank.
- References must not be blank or duplicated.
- All supplied timestamps must be timezone-aware.
- Completed and failed investigations require a completion timestamp.
- Completion time must not precede start time.
- References must resolve to existing records when validated by the service layer.

## Shared execution conventions

- All schema models inherit from the shared `DomainModel`.
- Task execution status is separate from evidence confidence and event verification status.
- Task and investigation IDs identify records but do not prove that referenced records exist.
- Schema validation does not execute tasks, perform retries or persist state.
- State transitions, retry policies, task scheduling and concurrency control belong to the orchestration implementation.
- Changes to contracts must be accompanied by updated tests and documentation.

# Phase 5: Human Review & Audit Layer Specification

This document details the architectural design, workflow contracts, domain models, REST/WebSocket interfaces, CLI commands, and export capabilities implemented for **Phase 5 (Human Review & Audit Layer)** of the VIGIL Video Forensics platform.

---

## 1. Architectural Overview & Workflow Integration

Phase 5 completes the end-to-end VIGIL forensic lifecycle specified in [VIGIL_vison_modelContext.txt](file:///D:/Major%20Project/VIGIL/documentation/architectures/VIGIL_vison_modelContext.txt) (Stages 8 & 9: *Report Writer* and *Human Review, Audit, and Report Versions*).

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    actor Investigator as Human Investigator
    participant API as FastAPI REST & Telemetry
    participant Svc as InvestigationService
    participant Repo as SqliteInvestigationRepository
    participant Slicer as ClipSlicer (PyAV/FFmpeg)
    participant Exporter as ReportExporter (HTML/PDF)

    Note over Investigator,Repo: Step 1: Interactive Event Review
    Investigator->>API: POST /api/v1/investigations/{id}/events/{event_id}/review<br/>(status="confirmed" | "rejected", reviewer, notes, edits)
    API->>Svc: review_event(...)
    Svc->>Repo: Update Event & Persist AuditLog
    Svc-->>API: Broadcast "event_reviewed" telemetry
    API-->>Investigator: 200 OK (Updated Event + AuditLog)

    Note over Investigator,Repo: Step 2: Finalize Report & Versioning
    Investigator->>API: POST /api/v1/investigations/{id}/report/finalize<br/>(finalized_by, notes, auto_slice_clips=True)
    API->>Svc: finalize_report(...)
    opt Auto-slice incident clips
        Svc->>Slicer: Slice H.264 sub-clips for confirmed incidents
        Slicer-->>Repo: Register clip Artifacts with SHA-256 hashes
    end
    Svc->>Repo: Create Report v2 (status="finalized", clip_artifact_ids, audit log)
    API-->>Investigator: 200 OK (Finalized Report + AuditLog)

    Note over Investigator,Exporter: Step 3: Forensic Deliverable Export
    Investigator->>API: GET /api/v1/investigations/{id}/export/pdf (or /html)
    API->>Exporter: export_pdf(...) / export_html(...)
    Exporter-->>API: Binary PDF stream (%PDF-) / Standalone HTML
    API-->>Investigator: Download Court-Ready Deliverable
```

---

## 2. Core Components Implemented

### 2.1 AuditLog Domain Model ([`AuditLog`](file:///D:/Major%20Project/VIGIL/src/vigil/domain/audit.py#L14-L65))
Tracks immutable forensic provenance for all investigator interactions and system modifications:
* **`audit_id`**: Globally unique identifier (e.g. `audit-xxxxxxxx`).
* **`investigation_id`**: Reference to the case.
* **`action`**: Action type (`event_review`, `event_edit`, `report_finalize`, `report_regenerate`).
* **`actor`**: Investigator badge or username.
* **`timestamp`**: Timezone-aware UTC timestamp.
* **`target_type`**: `event`, `report`, `investigation`, or `artifact`.
* **`target_id`**: Target record identifier.
* **`changes`**: Key-value dictionary comparing previous state to new state: `{"status": {"old": "proposed", "new": "confirmed"}}`.
* **`notes`**: Investigator remarks, rationale, or legal justification.

### 2.2 Event Domain Enhancements ([`Event`](file:///D:/Major%20Project/VIGIL/src/vigil/domain/event.py#L11-L93))
* Added `start_seconds` and `end_seconds` with strict non-negative and order validation (`end_seconds >= start_seconds`).
* Added `reviewer`: Username/badge of reviewing investigator.
* Added `review_notes`: Justification or observations recorded during review.
* Added `reviewed_at`: UTC timestamp of review decision.

### 2.3 Report Versioning & Finalization ([`Report`](file:///D:/Major%20Project/VIGIL/src/vigil/domain/report.py#L11-L54))
* **`version`**: Monotonically increasing version counter (`1`, `2`, `3`).
* **`status`**: Lifecycle state (`draft`, `in_review`, `finalized`).
* **`clip_artifact_ids`**: List of verified incident video sub-clips embedded in the report.
* **`notes`**: Concluding investigator remarks.
* **`finalized_at`** & **`finalized_by`**: Chain of custody sign-off metadata.

### 2.4 Persistent SQLite Audit Schema ([`SqliteInvestigationRepository`](file:///D:/Major%20Project/VIGIL/src/vigil/storage/sqlite_repository.py#L127-L139))
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS audit_logs (
    audit_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    investigation_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    target_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    target_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    action TEXT NOT NULL,
    actor TEXT NOT NULL,
    timestamp TEXT NOT NULL,
    payload TEXT NOT NULL
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_audit_inv ON audit_logs(investigation_id);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_audit_target ON audit_logs(target_id);
```

### 2.5 Report Exporter ([`ReportExporter`](file:///D:/Major%20Project/VIGIL/src/vigil/services/exporter.py#L39-L560))
* **HTML Deliverable (`export_html`)**:
  * Standalone, responsive dark/light forensic interface.
  * Executive summary, case objective, and metrics overview cards.
  * Chronological event timeline with status pills (`CONFIRMED`, `REJECTED`, `PROPOSED`, `UNDER_REVIEW`).
  * Embedded playable HTML5 video players (`<video controls preload="metadata">`) streaming incident sub-clips.
  * Complete evidence assessment table and chronological audit trail table.
  * Clean `@media print` CSS rules for high-contrast paper/PDF printing directly from web browsers.
* **PDF Deliverable (`export_pdf`)**:
  * Generated via ReportLab Platypus engine.
  * Custom two-pass `NumberedCanvas` computing dynamic running headers and footers with total page counts (`Page X of Y`).
  * Structured metadata cards, executive callout boxes, event timeline tables with colored status indicators.
  * Incident clip tables detailing media asset references, intervals, storage paths, and SHA-256 integrity checksums.
  * Complete investigator review decision table and audit trail.

---

## 3. REST API Endpoints Summary

| Method | Endpoint | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `POST` | `/api/v1/investigations/{id}/events/{event_id}/review` | Submit human verification (`confirmed`/`rejected`) or status update with rationale | `200 OK` |
| `PATCH` | `/api/v1/investigations/{id}/events/{event_id}` | Directly edit event attributes (description, timestamps, category) with audit tracking | `200 OK` |
| `GET` | `/api/v1/investigations/{id}/events` | List events, with optional filter `?status=confirmed\|rejected\|proposed` | `200 OK` |
| `GET` | `/api/v1/investigations/{id}/events/{event_id}` | Retrieve single event domain record | `200 OK` |
| `POST` | `/api/v1/investigations/{id}/events/{event_id}/clip` | Manually slice a playable sub-clip around an event's time interval | `200 OK` |
| `GET` | `/api/v1/investigations/{id}/audit` | Query audit trail with optional filters (`target_id`, `actor`, `action`) | `200 OK` |
| `POST` | `/api/v1/investigations/{id}/report/finalize` | Finalize report, increment version, slice clips, and update status to `finalized` | `200 OK` |
| `GET` | `/api/v1/investigations/{id}/export/html` | Export interactive HTML forensic report with embedded playable incident clips | `200 OK` |
| `GET` | `/api/v1/investigations/{id}/export/pdf` | Export formal publication-grade PDF deliverable with audit chain of custody | `200 OK` |
| `GET` | `/api/v1/investigations/{id}/report?format=html\|pdf` | Format query support directly on the report endpoint | `200 OK` |
| `GET` | `/api/v1/investigations/{id}/artifacts/{artifact_id}/content` | Stream or download video sub-clips, audio tracks, and JPEG frame artifacts | `200 OK` |

---

## 4. CLI Workflow Commands

The `vigil` CLI script supports all Phase 5 investigator workflows:

```bash
# 1. Start API Server
vigil serve --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000

# 2. List Events for an Investigation
vigil review list-events inv-001 [--status proposed]

# 3. Review / Confirm an Event
vigil review event inv-001 evt-001 --status confirmed --reviewer "badge-402" --notes "Verified breach on cam 2"

# 4. Review / Reject a False Positive
vigil review event inv-001 evt-002 --status rejected --reviewer "badge-402" --notes "Stray animal trigger"

# 5. Inspect Audit Trail
vigil audit inv-001

# 6. Finalize Report & Slice Incident Clips
vigil finalize-report inv-001 --finalized-by "detective-carter" --notes "Final court submission"

# 7. Export Court-Ready Deliverables
vigil export inv-001 --format html --output report.html
vigil export inv-001 --format pdf --output report.pdf
```

CODE:
# Investigation Management and Coverage Schemas

## 1. Purpose

This document defines the domain contracts for managing investigations, recording examination coverage, and producing structured reports in VIGIL.

The schemas build on the existing source/media and evidence schemas. They store references to existing objects rather than duplicating their contents.

## 2. Investigation

**Source file:** `src/vigil/domain/investigation.py`

**Purpose:** Represents an investigation, its objective, its scope, and the media/source references associated with it.

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `investigation_id` | `str` | Yes | Unique identifier for the investigation. Must not be blank. |
| `title` | `str` | Yes | Human-readable investigation title. |
| `objective` | `str` | Yes | Describes what the investigation aims to establish. |
| `scope_description` | `str` | Yes | Describes the investigation boundaries and constraints. |
| `media_asset_ids` | `list[str]` | No | IDs of media assets associated with the investigation. Defaults to an empty list. |
| `source_reference_ids` | `list[str]` | No | IDs of source references associated with the investigation. Defaults to an empty list. |
| `created_at` | `datetime` | No | Time the investigation record was created. Defaults to the current UTC time and must be timezone-aware. |
| `created_by` | `str \| None` | No | Optional identifier for the user or component that created the investigation. |

### Validation rules

- Required text fields cannot be blank.
- Media and source reference IDs must be nonblank and unique within their respective lists.
- `created_at` must be timezone-aware.
- Unknown fields are rejected through the shared `DomainModel` configuration.
- The schema does not currently define investigation lifecycle status.

## 3. CoverageRecord

**Source file:** `src/vigil/domain/coverage.py`

**Purpose:** Records whether a source or time interval was examined and captures the outcome and associated artifacts.

### CoverageStatus values

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `examined` | The examination was completed successfully. |
| `partial` | Only part of the intended examination was completed. |
| `failed` | The examination attempt failed. |
| `not_examined` | The source or interval has not been examined. |

### Attributes

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `coverage_id` | `str` | Yes | Unique identifier for this coverage record. |
| `investigation_id` | `str` | Yes | Identifier of the associated investigation. |
| `source_reference_id` | `str` | Yes | Identifier of the source being examined. |
| `status` | `CoverageStatus` | Yes | Outcome of the examination. |
| `interval_start` | `datetime \| None` | No | Start of the interval being examined. |
| `interval_end` | `datetime \| None` | No | End of the interval being examined. |
| `examined_at` | `datetime \| None` | No | Time the examination was recorded as completed or attempted. |
| `notes` | `str \| None` | No | Explanation of limitations, failures, or other relevant details. |
| `artifact_ids` | `list[str]` | No | IDs of artifacts produced by the examination. Defaults to an empty list. |

### Validation rules

- Required identifiers must not be blank.
- Supplied timestamps must be timezone-aware.
- `interval_start` and `interval_end` must either both be provided or both be omitted.
- The interval end cannot precede the interval start.
- `examined` status requires `examined_at`.
- Artifact IDs must be nonblank and unique.
- Optional notes cannot be blank when supplied.

### Interpretation rule

Coverage records describe examination activity. They do not establish that a source contains no relevant events. In particular, `not_examined` must not be interpreted as a successful examination with no findings.

## 4. Report

**Source file:** `src/vigil/domain/report.py`

**Purpose:** Represents structured investigation findings and references the evidence and coverage records supporting them.

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `report_id` | `str` | Yes | Unique report identifier. |
| `investigation_id` | `str` | Yes | Identifier of the investigation summarized by the report. |
| `title` | `str` | Yes | Human-readable report title. |
| `summary` | `str` | Yes | High-level summary of findings. |
| `observation_ids` | `list[str]` | No | IDs of supporting observations. Defaults to an empty list. |
| `event_ids` | `list[str]` | No | IDs of supporting events. Defaults to an empty list. |
| `assessment_ids` | `list[str]` | No | IDs of evidence assessments used in the report. Defaults to an empty list. |
| `coverage_ids` | `list[str]` | No | IDs of relevant coverage records. Defaults to an empty list. |
| `generated_at` | `datetime` | No | Report generation time. Defaults to the current UTC time and must be timezone-aware. |
| `generated_by` | `str` | Yes | Identifier of the user or component that generated the report. |

### Validation rules

- Required text fields cannot be blank.
- Each evidence/coverage reference list must contain unique, nonblank IDs.
- `generated_at` must be timezone-aware.
- Unknown fields are rejected through `DomainModel`.

## 5. Dependencies and boundaries

The schemas reference existing domain concepts:

- `Investigation` references media assets and source references.
- `CoverageRecord` references an investigation, a source reference, and optional artifacts.
- `Report` references an investigation, observations, events, evidence assessments, and coverage records.

These are identifier references, not automatic database relationships. Referential existence checks, persistence, and cross-record consistency are responsibilities of a later repository or application-service layer.

## 6. Intentionally deferred

The following are outside this schema set's current scope:

- Investigation lifecycle transitions.
- Automatic calculation of examination completeness.
- Rules for deciding whether coverage is sufficient.
- Report rendering to PDF, HTML, or other formats.
- Validation that every referenced ID exists in persistent storage.
- Report versioning and approval workflows.

These capabilities can be added when the corresponding application behavior is designed.

# VIGIL Orchestration — Executor, Transitions and Service Specification

**Status:** Initial implementation specification
**Scope:** `TaskExecutor` and its exceptions (`executor.py`), `transition_state` and the lifecycle table (`transitions.py`), `OrchestrationService` and its outcome/exception types (`service.py`)

**Depends on:** `TaskRequest`, `TaskResult`, `TaskStatus`, `ErrorDetail` (`task_types.py`) and `InvestigationState`, `InvestigationStatus` (`investigation_state.py`).

## 0. How the three modules fit together

```
caller
  │  execute_task(state, request)
  ▼
OrchestrationService  (service.py)      ← owns the workflow: preconditions, state updates, failure policy
  │ uses                       │ uses
  ▼                            ▼
TaskExecutor (executor.py)     transition_state (transitions.py)
  routes request → worker        validates lifecycle changes
  validates the TaskResult       returns a NEW InvestigationState
  │
  ▼
TaskWorker implementations (not yet written)
```

- The **executor** knows about tasks only. It never sees `InvestigationState`.
- **Transitions** know about investigation lifecycle only. They never see tasks.
- The **service** is the only module that knows about both and decides what a task outcome means for the investigation.
- All state objects are immutable (`DomainModel` is `frozen=True`). Every "update" returns a new object that is re-validated through Pydantic.

---

## 1. executor.py

**Python file:** `src/vigil/orchestration/executor.py`

### 1.1 TaskWorker (Protocol)

| Member | Signature | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `execute` | `(request: TaskRequest) -> TaskResult` | Executes one task synchronously and returns its final result. |

Any object with a matching `execute` method is a valid worker (structural typing; no inheritance required).

### 1.2 Exceptions

| Exception | Base | Raised when |
|---|---|---|
| `ExecutorError` | `Exception` | Base class for all executor errors. |
| `UnknownTaskTypeError` | `ExecutorError` | No worker is registered for the request's `task_type`. |
| `TaskResultContractError` | `ExecutorError` | A worker returns something that violates the result contract. |
| `WorkerExecutionError` | `ExecutorError` | **Raised by workers** to report an expected, handled execution failure. The executor does not raise or catch it. |

### 1.3 TaskExecutor

| Attribute / method | Description |
|---|---|
| `_workers: dict[str, TaskWorker]` | Private registry mapping a normalized task type to its worker. |
| `register(task_type, worker) -> None` | Registers exactly one worker for a task type. |
| `execute(request) -> TaskResult` | Looks up the worker, runs it and validates the returned result. |

**`register` rules**

- The task type is stripped of surrounding whitespace before use.
- A blank task type raises `ValueError`.
- Registering a second worker for the same (normalized) task type raises `ValueError`; workers are never silently replaced.

**`execute` rules (in order)**

1. Look up the worker by `request.task_type`. If none exists → `UnknownTaskTypeError`.
2. Call `worker.execute(request)`.
3. The return value must be a `TaskResult` instance → otherwise `TaskResultContractError`.
4. `result.task_id` must equal `request.task_id` → otherwise `TaskResultContractError`.
5. `result.status` must be final (`completed`, `partial` or `failed`). `pending` or `running` → `TaskResultContractError`, because this executor is synchronous.
6. Return the result unchanged.

**What the executor deliberately does not do**

- It does not catch worker exceptions. `WorkerExecutionError` and any unexpected exception propagate to the caller.
- It does not retry, time, log, schedule or run tasks concurrently.
- It does not check that `output_refs` point to existing records.
- It does not inspect `TaskResult.error` beyond what `TaskResult` already validates for itself.

---

## 2. transitions.py

**Python file:** `src/vigil/orchestration/transitions.py`

### 2.1 InvalidStateTransitionError

Subclass of `ValueError`. Raised when a requested lifecycle change is not in the allowed table.

### 2.2 Allowed transitions

| From | Allowed next states |
|---|---|
| `created` | `running`, `failed` |
| `running` | `paused`, `completed`, `failed` |
| `paused` | `running`, `failed` |
| `completed` | *(none — terminal)* |
| `failed` | *(none — terminal)* |

Consequences worth knowing:

- Same-state transitions (for example `running → running`) are **not** allowed and raise.
- `created → completed` is not allowed; an investigation must pass through `running`.
- `completed` and `failed` are terminal and cannot be reopened.

The table is the module-private constant `_ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS`.

### 2.3 transition_state

| Parameter | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `state` | `InvestigationState` | Current state. Never modified. |
| `new_status` | `InvestigationStatus` | Requested status. |
| **Returns** | `InvestigationState` | A new, validated state. |

**Behavior**

1. If `new_status` is not allowed from `state.status` → `InvalidStateTransitionError` (message includes both status values).
2. Take a single `now = datetime.now(UTC)`.
3. Always set `status = new_status` and `updated_at = now`.
4. When moving to `running` **and** `started_at` is `None`, set `started_at = now`. Resuming from `paused` therefore keeps the original `started_at`.
5. When moving to `completed` or `failed`, set `completed_at = now`.
6. Build the result with `InvestigationState.model_validate(state.model_dump() | updates)`, so every `InvestigationState` validator runs again. (`model_copy(update=...)` is intentionally avoided because it skips validation.)

---

## 3. service.py

**Python file:** `src/vigil/orchestration/service.py`

### 3.1 TaskExecutionOutcome

Frozen, slotted `dataclass` returned by `execute_task`.

| Attribute | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `state` | `InvestigationState` | The investigation state after the task was processed. |
| `task_result` | `TaskResult` | The result returned by the worker, or a synthesized failure result. |

### 3.2 Exceptions

| Exception | Base | Raised when |
|---|---|---|
| `OrchestrationServiceError` | `RuntimeError` | Base class for service errors. |
| `InvalidInvestigationStateError` | `OrchestrationServiceError` | The investigation's status does not allow the requested operation. |
| `DuplicateTaskError` | `OrchestrationServiceError` | `request.task_id` is already in `state.task_ids`. |

`UnknownTaskTypeError`, `TaskResultContractError` and `InvalidStateTransitionError` are **not** wrapped; they pass through the service unchanged.

### 3.3 OrchestrationService

Constructed with a `TaskExecutor`. It holds no investigation state itself; state is passed in and returned.

#### `execute_task(state, request) -> TaskExecutionOutcome`

**Preconditions**

- `state.status` must be `created` or `running`; otherwise `InvalidInvestigationStateError`. (`paused`, `completed` and `failed` investigations cannot accept tasks.)
- `request.task_id` must not already be in `state.task_ids`; otherwise `DuplicateTaskError`.

**Flow**

1. Record `started_at = now`.
2. If the state is `created`, transition it to `running` (this also sets the investigation's `started_at`). If it is already `running`, use it as is.
3. Call `executor.execute(request)`.
   - `UnknownTaskTypeError` or `TaskResultContractError` → re-raised. These are configuration or contract bugs and must stay visible. The input state is untouched, so the caller still holds the pre-call state.
   - `WorkerExecutionError` → converted into a `TaskResult` with `status=failed`, `output_refs=[]`, `error=ErrorDetail(code="WORKER_EXECUTION_ERROR", message="The worker reported an execution failure.", retryable=False)`, and the service's `started_at` / `completed_at` timestamps.
4. Build a new state containing `task_ids + [request.task_id]` and `updated_at = finished_at`.
5. If the task result is `failed`, transition that state to `failed` (setting `completed_at`). Otherwise return it as is; `completed` and `partial` tasks leave the investigation `running`.
6. Return `TaskExecutionOutcome(state, task_result)`.

**Failure policy (current)**

| Task result | Investigation after the call |
|---|---|
| `completed` | `running` |
| `partial` | `running` |
| `failed` | `failed` (terminal) |

A single failed task therefore ends the whole investigation. `ErrorDetail.retryable` is recorded but not acted on.

#### `complete_investigation(state) -> InvestigationState`

- Only a `running` investigation can be completed; otherwise `InvalidInvestigationStateError`.
- Delegates to `transition_state(state, COMPLETED)`.

---

## 4. Known limitations and deferred work

- **No output recording.** `TaskResult.output_refs` are not written into `InvestigationState.observation_ids` or `event_ids`.
- **No pause, resume or explicit fail operations** on the service. The `paused` state exists in the lifecycle table but nothing in the service reaches it.
- **Worker failure details are discarded.** The message of a caught `WorkerExecutionError` is replaced by a generic message; the error code is always `WORKER_EXECUTION_ERROR` and `retryable` is always `false`.
- **Unexpected worker exceptions** (anything other than `WorkerExecutionError`) propagate out of the service without being recorded in state.
- **No persistence.** State lives only in the returned objects; nothing is stored.
- **Synchronous, single-task execution only.** No retries, scheduling, timeouts or concurrency.
- **No existence checks** on `input_refs` or `output_refs`; these belong to the future repository/application layer (see `shared_contracts.md`, section 8).
- **No concrete workers** are registered anywhere yet.

## 5. Testing expectations

Changes to these modules should be accompanied by unit tests covering at least:

- Executor: blank/duplicate registration, unknown task type, non-`TaskResult` return, mismatched `task_id`, `pending`/`running` result, normal pass-through.
- Transitions: every allowed and disallowed pair, `started_at` set only on the first `running`, `completed_at` set on terminal states, input state not mutated.
- Service: the three task outcomes, `created → running` on first task, duplicate task ID, invalid state, `WorkerExecutionError` conversion, pass-through of configuration/contract errors, `complete_investigation` guard.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

CODE:
# VIGIL Shared Contracts and Validation Rules

## 1. Purpose

This document defines conventions that apply across VIGIL domain schemas and execution contracts. Individual schemas must follow these rules rather than independently defining the meaning of identifiers, timestamps, confidence values, references, provenance, and validation.

**Implementation status:** The shared primitive types are implemented in `src/vigil/domain/common.py`. The source/media, evidence, investigation-management, and orchestration schema groups have been migrated to these types, with unit tests covering their validation behavior. Cross-record existence checks, persistence guarantees, and workflow-level state transitions remain responsibilities of later layers.

## 2. ID conventions

### Decision

- Represent identifiers as nonblank strings.
- Use stable identifiers for persisted objects; an object's ID must not change when its descriptive fields change.
- Human-readable prefixes such as `media-`, `event-`, and `task-` may be used as a readability convention.
- A prefix is not a guarantee of uniqueness.
- Do not use list positions, display labels, timestamps, or mutable attributes as persistent identifiers.
- Do not assume IDs from different entity types are globally unique unless the storage layer explicitly guarantees this.

### Validation

`Identifier` is defined in `src/vigil/domain/common.py`. It strips surrounding whitespace and rejects blank values.

The mechanism that generates IDs and guarantees uniqueness in persistent storage is a responsibility of a later application or persistence layer.

## 3. Timestamp and interval conventions

### Real-world timestamps

- Use timezone-aware datetimes for real-world timestamps.
- Use UTC as the default timezone for timestamps generated by VIGIL.
- Preserve the distinction between when an event occurred and when VIGIL observed, processed, or recorded it.
- Unknown timestamps should remain unknown; do not substitute the current time for an unknown event time.
- Do not use naive datetimes to represent real-world time.

The reusable `Timestamp` type uses Pydantic's timezone-aware datetime validation.

### Media-relative time

A media offset is a position relative to a media asset, such as a number of seconds from the beginning of a video. It is not a real-world datetime.

When both a media-relative position and an estimated real-world time are required, represent them as separate fields and document their relationship.

### Interval boundaries

Use half-open intervals: `[start, end)`.

- The start is included.
- The end is excluded.
- The end must not precede the start.
- If both boundaries are required, both must be present.
- A zero-length interval is empty.
- Unknown boundaries should be represented explicitly as unknown where the schema permits them.

This convention allows adjacent intervals to meet at a boundary without overlapping.

## 4. Confidence and uncertainty

### Confidence

- A confidence score is numeric and normalized from `0.0` to `1.0`.
- The score must be finite.
- Confidence is optional when the producing component cannot justify a score.
- A score is not automatically a calibrated probability.
- The meaning of a score must be documented for the component or model that produces it.

The shared `ConfidenceScore` type validates the numeric range and rejects non-finite values.

### Uncertainty and evidence quality

Confidence, uncertainty, and evidence quality are related but distinct:

- **Confidence:** How strongly a component supports its own output.
- **Uncertainty:** What remains unknown, ambiguous, or unresolved.
- **Evidence quality:** How suitable the supporting evidence is for the relevant assessment.

Do not encode all three concepts in a single numeric field. Use explicit assessments and rationale where appropriate.

## 5. Reference rules

- Store identifiers when one schema refers to another object.
- Avoid copying the full referenced object into every dependent schema.
- Use consistent ID types for references and target objects.
- Reject blank reference IDs.
- Reject duplicate references when list membership represents a set of related objects.
- Do not assume that a syntactically valid ID points to an existing object.
- Check referential existence at the repository or application-service boundary, where related records can be looked up.

The shared `ReferenceIds` type validates nonblank, unique reference IDs.

Reference lists should retain ordering only when ordering has meaning to the domain. Otherwise, their meaning is membership rather than sequence.

## 6. Provenance rules

Provenance records how a result was produced and what it depends on.

Where applicable, retain:

- Producer type and name.
- Producer or model version.
- Processing or analysis method.
- Input artifact IDs.
- Parent observation IDs.
- Creation timestamp.
- Relevant transformation or derivation information.

Derived observations, artifacts, and events should retain links to their inputs. Correlation must not erase the distinction between an original source, a derived result, and an interpretation.

Provenance supports traceability but does not, by itself, prove that a result is correct.

## 7. Schema versioning

### Contract evolution

- Treat externally exchanged schemas as versioned contracts.
- Introduce an explicit schema version at a serialized message, API, or persisted-document boundary where compatibility must be managed.
- Do not add a redundant version field to every internal model without a concrete need.
- Prefer additive optional fields when they preserve existing semantics.
- Treat field removal, type changes, stricter validation, and changes in field meaning as potentially breaking changes.
- Document migration requirements before deploying breaking changes.
- Test compatibility between producers and consumers when a contract changes.

The initial contract baseline is version `1.0` for documentation and compatibility planning. This does not imply that every existing model currently carries a serialized `schema_version` field.

## 8. Validation rules

Validation should happen at multiple boundaries:

1. **Schema validation:** Types, required fields, nonblank IDs, confidence ranges, timezone-aware timestamps, and local interval rules.
2. **Cross-reference validation:** Whether referenced IDs exist and whether relationships between records are valid.
3. **Workflow validation:** Whether a task result or state transition is consistent with the workflow.
4. **Persistence validation:** Uniqueness, transaction consistency, and storage-level integrity.
5. **Semantic validation:** Whether a finding is adequately supported by evidence and whether uncertainty is accurately represented.

Do not attempt to solve every layer in a single Pydantic model. Keep each rule at the boundary that has enough information to enforce it correctly.

## 9. Shared implementation

Shared types are defined in:

`src/vigil/domain/common.py`

- `Identifier`: nonblank string identifier.
- `Timestamp`: timezone-aware datetime.
- `ConfidenceScore`: finite numeric score in the range from `0.0` to `1.0`.
- `ReferenceIds`: list of nonblank, unique reference identifiers.

New schemas should use these types where their semantics match. Existing schemas should be migrated incrementally, with tests confirming that their intended behavior remains unchanged.

### Initial schema migration: source and media

The source/media schema group uses the shared types as follows:

| Schema | Shared types applied |
|---|---|
| `Camera` | `Identifier` for `camera_id` |
| `MediaAsset` | `Identifier` for `media_asset_id` and optional `camera_id`; `Timestamp` for `recorded_at` |
| `SourceReference` | `Identifier` for `media_asset_id`; `Timestamp` for `source_timestamp` |
| `Artifact` | `Identifier` for `artifact_id`; `Timestamp` for `created_at` |

Domain-specific validation remains in each schema. Examples include IANA timezone validation, finite media positions, positive-length intervals, SHA-256 checksums, and artifact/media-type compatibility.

This migration does not verify that a referenced camera or media asset exists in persistent storage. Cross-record existence checks remain the responsibility of a future repository or application-service layer.

### Evidence schema migration

The evidence schemas use the shared types as follows:

| Schema | Shared types applied |
|---|---|
| `Provenance` | `Timestamp`, `ReferenceIds` |
| `Observation` | `Identifier`, `ConfidenceScore`, `ReferenceIds` |
| `Entity` | `Identifier`, `ReferenceIds` |
| `Event` | `Identifier`, `Timestamp`, `ReferenceIds` |
| `EventLink` | `Identifier`, `ConfidenceScore`, `Timestamp` |
| `EvidenceAssessment` | `Identifier`, `Timestamp` |

Domain-specific validation remains in place, including required supporting observations, event time ordering, distinct endpoints for event links, and finite entity attribute values.

Confidence values use the shared finite range from `0.0` to `1.0`. Reference lists use the shared validation rules for blank and duplicate IDs, while fields requiring at least one reference retain their minimum-length constraints.

The schemas validate the structure and syntax of references, but they do not verify that referenced records exist in persistent storage. Cross-record existence checks remain the responsibility of a repository or application-service layer.

## 10. Deferred implementation

The following are not fully implemented by this document or the shared types alone:

- Persistent ID generation and uniqueness guarantees.
- Automatic conversion between media-relative and real-world time.
- Referential existence checks across stored records.
- Provenance propagation through every processing stage.
- API or database migration tooling for schema versions.
- Cross-record and workflow-level validation.

These responsibilities should be implemented when the relevant repository, pipeline, and integration contracts are introduced.


### Investigation management and coverage migration

The investigation management schemas use the shared types as follows:

| Schema | Shared types applied |
|---|---|
| `Investigation` | `Identifier`, `Timestamp`, `ReferenceIds` |
| `CoverageRecord` | `Identifier`, `Timestamp`, `ReferenceIds` |
| `Report` | `Identifier`, `Timestamp`, `ReferenceIds` |

Domain-specific validation remains in place, including nonblank descriptive text, paired coverage interval endpoints, interval ordering, the requirement for an examination timestamp when coverage is marked as examined, and nonblank optional notes.

The shared `Identifier` and `ReferenceIds` types normalize surrounding whitespace and reject blank identifiers. Reference lists also reject duplicate identifiers after normalization. The shared `Timestamp` type requires timezone-aware datetime values.

The schemas validate the structure and syntax of references, but they do not verify that referenced records exist in persistent storage. Cross-record existence checks remain the responsibility of a repository or application-service layer.



### Execution and orchestration migration

The orchestration schemas use the shared types as follows:

| Schema | Shared types applied |
|---|---|
| `InvestigationState` | `Identifier`, `ReferenceIds`, `Timestamp` |
| `TaskRequest` | `Identifier`, `ReferenceIds`, `Timestamp` |
| `TaskResult` | `Identifier`, `ReferenceIds`, `Timestamp` |
| `ModelOutput` | `Identifier`, `ReferenceIds`, `Timestamp`, `ConfidenceScore` |

`ErrorDetail` retains its domain-specific validation for nonblank error text, finite retry delays, and retry-policy consistency.

The task and investigation state schemas retain their lifecycle rules, including required error details for failed tasks, prohibition of errors on completed tasks, terminal investigation timestamps, and chronological ordering of start and completion timestamps.

The shared contracts validate individual values and reference-list structure. They do not execute tasks, manage transitions over time, or verify the existence of referenced records. Those responsibilities belong to the orchestration service, scheduler, or repository layer.

# VIGIL Layer 1 — Source and Media Schema Specification

**Status:** Initial implementation specification  
**Scope:** Camera, MediaAsset, SourceReference and Artifact

## 1. Camera

**Python file:** `src/vigil/domain/camera.py`

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `camera_id` | `str` | Yes | Stable unique identifier for the camera. |
| `name` | `str` | Yes | Human-readable camera name. |
| `location_label` | `str \| None` | No | Physical location of the camera, if known. |
| `timezone` | `str \| None` | No | IANA timezone associated with the camera, if known. |
| `clock_offset_seconds` | `float \| None` | No | Correction applied to the camera-reported clock to estimate reference time. |

**Validation rules**

- IDs and names must not be empty.
- Timezones must be valid IANA timezone names.
- Clock offsets must be finite numbers.
- Unknown metadata must remain explicitly optional.

## 2. MediaAsset

**Python file:** `src/vigil/domain/media_asset.py`

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `media_asset_id` | `str` | Yes | Stable unique identifier for the original media. |
| `media_type` | `video \| audio \| image` | Yes | Type of original media. |
| `storage_uri` | `str` | Yes | Location or storage key for the original media. |
| `camera_id` | `str \| None` | No | Identifier of the producing camera, if known. |
| `duration_seconds` | `float \| None` | No | Positive duration in seconds, if applicable and known. |
| `recorded_at` | `datetime \| None` | No | Timezone-aware wall-clock time corresponding to the recording's beginning. |
| `sha256` | `str \| None` | No | SHA-256 checksum of the original media, when available. |

**Validation rules**

- IDs and storage references must not be empty.
- Media type must be one of the declared values.
- Duration must be positive and finite when supplied.
- Recording timestamps must be timezone-aware.
- SHA-256 checksums must contain exactly 64 hexadecimal characters.
- The referenced camera must exist when `camera_id` is supplied.

## 3. SourceReference

**Python file:** `src/vigil/domain/source_reference.py`

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `media_asset_id` | `str` | Yes | Identifier of the original media containing the evidence. |
| `start_seconds` | `float` | Yes | Inclusive start position in seconds on the original media timeline. |
| `end_seconds` | `float` | Yes | Exclusive end position in seconds on the original media timeline. |
| `source_timestamp` | `datetime \| None` | No | Timezone-aware wall-clock timestamp corresponding to `start_seconds`, when known. |

**Validation rules**

- Media IDs must not be empty.
- Timeline positions must be finite and non-negative.
- `end_seconds` must be strictly greater than `start_seconds`.
- Wall-clock timestamps must be timezone-aware.
- When media duration is known, the interval must not extend beyond it.
- Source references must resolve to existing media assets.

## 4. Artifact

**Python file:** `src/vigil/domain/artifact.py`

| Attribute | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `artifact_id` | `str` | Yes | Stable unique identifier for the derived artifact. |
| `artifact_type` | `frame \| crop \| clip \| audio_segment \| derived_media` | Yes | Kind of derived media. |
| `media_type` | `video \| audio \| image` | Yes | Actual media type of the stored artifact. |
| `storage_uri` | `str` | Yes | Location of the derived media. |
| `source_reference` | `SourceReference` | Yes | Original media and timeline interval from which the artifact was derived. |
| `sha256` | `str \| None` | No | SHA-256 checksum of the derived artifact, when available. |
| `created_at` | `datetime` | Yes | Timezone-aware timestamp when the artifact was created; defaults to UTC now. |

**Validation rules**

- Artifact IDs and storage references must not be empty.
- `frame` and `crop` require image media.
- `clip` requires video media.
- `audio_segment` requires audio media.
- `derived_media` may represent any declared media type.
- Creation timestamps must be timezone-aware.
- Checksums must contain exactly 64 hexadecimal characters when supplied.
- Source references must resolve to existing media assets.
- The source interval must be valid for the referenced media.

## Shared conventions

- IDs are opaque, non-empty strings; their generation mechanism is not specified by these schemas.
- Media-relative times are expressed in seconds.
- Source intervals use the half-open convention `[start_seconds, end_seconds)`.
- Wall-clock timestamps must include timezone information.
- Unknown information is represented with optional fields, not fabricated defaults.
- Cross-record relationships must be checked by the repository or service layer.
- Schema changes must be accompanied by tests and documentation updates.

# VIGIL — Project Context (Layer 1: Domain & Orchestration Contracts)

Agentic video-forensics system. **Currently implemented: Pydantic schemas only** (no persistence, services, agents, or API yet). `src/vigil/__init__.py` is still the `uv init` placeholder (`main()` prints hello).

## Stack & Tooling
- Python `>=3.12,<3.13` (pinned 3.12), managed with **uv** (`uv_build` backend, `src/` layout, module `vigil`, script `vigil = vigil:main`)
- Runtime deps: `pydantic>=2.7,<3` (locked 2.14.1), `tzdata`
- Dev deps: `pytest>=8.0` (locked 9.1.1), `ruff>=0.9` (locked 0.17.0)
- Commands: `uv sync` · `uv run pytest tests/unit/domain/test_source_media_schemas.py -v` · `uv run ruff check [--fix] src/vigil/domain tests/unit/domain` · `uv run ruff format [--check] ...`
- Authors: KedarHiremath, kriti03ka

## Structure
```
src/vigil/
  domain/         base, common, camera, media_asset, source_reference, artifact,
                  provenance, observation, entity, event, event_link,
                  evidence_assessment, investigation, coverage, report
  orchestration/  task_types, investigation_state
documentation/architectures/   5 spec .md files (one per schema group + shared_contracts)
tests/unit/domain/             (tests exist per docs; not provided)
```
`domain/__init__.py` exports only `CoverageRecord`, `CoverageStatus`, `Investigation`, `Report`.

## Shared Foundation
- **`DomainModel`** (base for all): `extra="forbid"`, `str_strip_whitespace`, `validate_assignment`, **`frozen=True`** (immutable).
- **`common.py` types:** `Identifier` (stripped, nonblank str) · `Timestamp` (`AwareDatetime`) · `ConfidenceScore` (float 0–1, no inf/NaN) · `ReferenceIds` (list of `Identifier`, nonblank + unique after strip).

## Conventions (shared_contracts)
- IDs: opaque nonblank strings; prefixes (`media-`, `event-`) are readability only; existence is **not** validated by schemas.
- Time: tz-aware only, UTC default; media-relative seconds kept separate from wall-clock; unknown stays `None` (never fabricate). Intervals are half-open `[start, end)`.
- Confidence ≠ uncertainty ≠ evidence quality; score is not a calibrated probability.
- Store ID references, not copies of objects. Provenance aids traceability, doesn't prove correctness.
- Validation layers: schema → cross-reference → workflow → persistence → semantic. Each rule lives at the boundary that can enforce it.
- Versioning: baseline contract `1.0` (documentation only; no `schema_version` field on models). Prefer additive optional fields.

## Domain Schemas
**Source/media**
| Model | Key fields | Validation |
|---|---|---|
| `Camera` | `camera_id`, `name`, `location_label?`, `timezone?`, `clock_offset_seconds?` | valid IANA tz; finite offset |
| `MediaAsset` | `media_asset_id`, `media_type` (video/audio/image), `storage_uri`, `camera_id?`, `duration_seconds?`, `recorded_at?`, `sha256?` | duration >0 & finite; sha256 = 64 hex; URI nonblank |
| `SourceReference` | `media_asset_id`, `start_seconds` (≥0), `end_seconds` (>0), `source_timestamp?` | finite; `end > start` |
| `Artifact` | `artifact_id`, `artifact_type` (frame/crop/clip/audio_segment/derived_media), `media_type`, `storage_uri`, `source_reference`, `sha256?`, `created_at` | frame/crop→image, clip→video, audio_segment→audio; `derived_media` any |

**Evidence/interpretation**
| Model | Key fields | Validation |
|---|---|---|
| `Provenance` | `producer_type` (vision_model/audio_model/rule/agent/human/other), `producer_name`, `producer_version?`, `method`, `input_artifact_ids`, `parent_observation_ids`, `created_at` | — |
| `Observation` | `observation_id`, `modality`, `observation_type`, `description`, `source_references` (≥1), `artifact_ids`, `confidence?`, `provenance` | ≥1 source ref |
| `Entity` | `entity_id`, `entity_type` (person/vehicle/animal/object/unknown), `label?` (not verified identity), `associated_observation_ids` (≥1), `attributes` (str/int/float/bool/None) | nonblank keys; finite floats |
| `Event` | `event_id`, `event_type`, `description`, `observation_ids` (≥1), `entity_ids`, `status` (proposed→under_review/confirmed/rejected; default proposed), `start_time?`, `end_time?` | `end ≥ start` |
| `EventLink` | `event_link_id`, `from_event_id`, `to_event_id`, `relationship_type` (before/after/overlaps/related_to/possible_same_incident), `rationale`, `confidence?`, `created_at` | no self-link |
| `EvidenceAssessment` | `assessment_id`, `target_type` (observation/event), `target_id`, `assessment_type` (evidence_quality/support_level/uncertainty), `rating` (insufficient/low/moderate/high), `rationale`, `assessed_by`, `created_at` | distinct from confidence |

**Investigation management**
| Model | Key fields | Validation |
|---|---|---|
| `Investigation` | `investigation_id`, `title`, `objective`, `scope_description`, `media_asset_ids`, `source_reference_ids`, `created_at`, `created_by?` | no lifecycle status here |
| `CoverageRecord` | `coverage_id`, `investigation_id`, `source_reference_id`, `status` (`CoverageStatus`: examined/partial/failed/not_examined), `interval_start?/end?`, `examined_at?`, `notes?`, `artifact_ids` | interval bounds both-or-neither; `end ≥ start`; `examined` requires `examined_at`; notes nonblank |
| `Report` | `report_id`, `investigation_id`, `title`, `summary`, `observation_ids`, `event_ids`, `assessment_ids`, `coverage_ids`, `generated_at`, `generated_by` | nonblank text |

Coverage records describe examination activity only — `not_examined` ≠ "examined, nothing found".

## Orchestration Schemas
| Model | Key fields | Validation |
|---|---|---|
| `TaskStatus` (enum) | pending, running, completed, partial, failed | execution status only, not truth/confidence |
| `ErrorDetail` | `code`, `message`, `retryable`, `retry_after_seconds?` (≥0, finite) | retry delay only if `retryable` |
| `TaskRequest` | `task_id`, `task_type`, `input_refs`, `parameters` (JSON dict), `requested_by?`, `created_at` | nonblank type |
| `TaskResult` | `task_id`, `status`, `output_refs`, `error?`, `started_at?`, `completed_at?` | failed ⇒ error required; completed ⇒ no error; `completed ≥ started` |
| `ModelOutput` | `output_id`, `producer_name`, `output_type`, `payload` (JSON dict), `confidence?`, `artifact_ids`, `created_at` | tool-specific payload validated elsewhere |
| `InvestigationStatus` (enum) | created, running, paused, completed, failed | — |
| `InvestigationState` | `investigation_id`, `status` (default created), `task_ids`, `observation_ids`, `event_ids`, `started_at?`, `updated_at`, `completed_at?` | completed/failed ⇒ `completed_at`; `completed ≥ started` |

## Explicitly Deferred (service/repository layer)
Referential existence checks · ID generation/uniqueness · media↔wall-clock conversion · interval-within-duration checks · state transitions, retries, scheduling · coverage sufficiency rules · report rendering/versioning/approval · schema-version migration tooling · provenance propagation · confirmation/verification process for events.

## Notes / Gaps
- Docs say `TaskResult` `partial` may carry an error; code allows it but doesn't require it.
- Docs list "interval must fit media duration" and "referenced IDs must exist" for `SourceReference`/`Artifact`/`MediaAsset`; these are **not** enforced in the schemas (by design, service layer).
- `InvestigationStatus` lives in `orchestration/investigation_state.py`; `Investigation` (domain) has no status.
- Test files, `README.md`, and any `Command_History`/`.gitignore` details beyond the above weren't relevant to the schema context.

CODE:
## The workflow every implementation model must understand

The following is the intended VIGIL architecture, expressed as a sequence of responsibilities and data flows. This should be included in the context given to any coding model, such as Antigravity, so it can plan future implementation stages without losing sight of the overall vision.
